Strategy brain, version 2.
1. Play solid, principled chess: develop, control the centre, castle early.
2. Before every move, list each of my pieces the enemy can capture (pawns and bishops included) and check whether it is defended. Bd6 into exd6 was a blunder.
3. Before every capture or move, check what it leaves hanging: the d-file, my queen's lines, and whether a recapture drops my queen.
4. Prefer simple recaptures and pawn wins over greedy trades that open files for enemy rooks.
5. When ahead, trade pieces and keep the king sheltered.
6. Check every enemy queen and rook for an undefended target before moving; A01 leaves pieces hanging late in games.
7. A01 opened 1.e4 e5 2.Nf3 Nc6 3.Bb5 a6 with a closed centre; it trades readily and blunders under tactical pressure.
8. Draw is worth half a point; accept only in clearly drawn positions.
